=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There are N mochi (rice cakes), arranged in ascending order of size.
The size of the i-th mochi (1\leq i\leq N) is A_i.
Given two mochi A and B, with sizes a and b respectively, you can make one kagamimochi (a stacked rice cake) by placing mochi A on top of mochi B if and only if a is at most half of b.
You are given Q integer pairs. Let (L_i, R_i) be the i-th pair (1\leq i\leq Q), and solve the following problem for each i:

Using only the R_i - L_i + 1 mochi from the L_i-th to the R_i-th, how many kagamimochi can you make simultaneously?  
More precisely, find the maximum non-negative integer K such that:

- Out of the R_i - L_i + 1 mochi from the L_i-th to the R_i-th, choose 2K mochi and form K pairs. For each pair, place one mochi on top of the other, to make K kagamimochi.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
A_1 A_2 \dotsc A_N
Q
L_1 R_1
L_2 R_2
\vdots
L_Q R_Q

Output

Print Q lines. The i-th line (1\leq i\leq Q) should contain the answer to the i-th query.

Constraints


- 2 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq A_i \leq 10^9 \ (1 \leq i \leq N)
- A_i \leq A_{i+1} \ (1 \leq i < N)
- 1 \leq Q \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq L_i < R_i \leq N \ (1 \leq i \leq Q)
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

11
1 1 2 3 4 4 7 10 11 12 20
5
2 5
3 8
7 11
1 2
1 11

Sample Output 1

2
3
1
0
5

The answers to each query are as follows. One possible way to make the kagamimochi is given for each query.

- The mochi sizes are (1, 2, 3, 4). You can make the two kagamimochi (1,3) and (2,4).
- The mochi sizes are (2, 3, 4, 4, 7, 10). You can make the three kagamimochi (2,4), (3,7), and (4,10).
- The mochi sizes are (7, 10, 11, 12, 20). You can make one kagamimochi (10,20).
- The mochi sizes are (1, 1). You cannot make any kagamimochi.
- The mochi sizes are (1, 1, 2, 3, 4, 4, 7, 10, 11, 12, 20). You can make five kagamimochi (1,2), (1,3), (4,10), (4,11), and (7,20).

Hence, print 2, 3, 1, 0, 5 in this order.

Sample Input 2

24
127 148 170 174 258 311 331 414 416 436 517 523 532 587 591 638 660 748 760 776 837 857 972 984
15
7 11
8 9
8 13
12 15
9 23
1 17
8 12
1 5
6 17
3 7
12 19
13 18
7 22
1 12
14 15

Sample Output 2

0
0
0
0
2
6
0
1
1
0
0
0
3
5
0

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Now, for each possible d, define "bad" positions x where 2A_x > A_{x+d}. Then condition for query with d is no bad x in [L, R-d]. We need minimal d >= ceil(M/2) with no bad in that interval.

Can we precompute for each x the set of d for which x is bad? Since A sorted. For fixed x, as d increases, A_{x+d} nondecreasing, so once 2A_x <= A_{x+d}, it remains true for larger d. Thus bad d for x are d < need[x]-x, where need[x] = lower_bound(2*A_x) (first index with A >= 2A_x). Specifically x is bad for d if x+d < need[x], i.e. d < need[x]-x. Let g[x] = need[x] - x. Since need[x] > x. Then for gap d, x is valid iff d >= g[x]. Nice! Because A_{x+d} >= 2A_x iff x+d >= need[x] iff d >= need[x]-x. Since A sorted. Great.

Thus condition for query with gap d: for all x in [L, R-d], d >= g[x]. Equivalently max_{x in [L, R-d]} g[x] <= d. Because if all g[x] <= d. Since g[x] maybe up to N+1-x.

So for query, need find minimal d >= ceil(M/2) such that d >= max_{x in [L, R-d]} g[x]. Let h(d) = max g[x] over x in [L, R-d]. Need d >= h(d). h(d) is nonincreasing? As d increases, R-d decreases (interval shrinks from left? Actually [L, R-d] removes rightmost elements, so max over smaller set can only decrease or stay. So h(d) nonincreasing as d increases. The condition d >= h(d): left side increases, right side nonincreases, so once true for some d, true for larger d? If d increases by 1, d increases, h(d) nonincreases, so yes monotonic. Thus minimal d can be binary searched! This is good. Need compute h(d) = range maximum of g over [L, R-d] quickly. Then binary search d in [ceil(M/2), M-1] maybe, with O(log M * RMQ). Range maximum static can be O(1) with sparse table or segment tree O(log N). Then per query O(log^2 N) if segment tree, or O(log N) with sparse table O(1). Need also handle K=0.

Let's outline code:

import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    it = iter(data)
    N = next(it)
    A = [next(it) for _ in range(N)]
    # compute g
    g = [0]*N
    p = 0
    for i in range(N):
        if p < i + 1:
            p = i + 1
        limit = A[i] * 2
        while p < N and A[p] < limit:
            p += 1
        g[i] = p - i

    # sparse table
    LOG = N.bit_length()
    st = [g]
    length = 1
    for k in range(1, LOG):
        prev = st[-1]
        half = 1 << (k-1)
        span = N - (1 << k) + 1
        if span <= 0:
            break
        # curr = [max(prev[i], prev[i+half]) for i in range(span)]
        curr = [0]*span
        # Maybe list comprehension faster:
        curr = [prev[i] if prev[i] >= prev[i+half] else prev[i+half] for i in range(span)]
        st.append(curr)
    # log table
    lg = [0]*(N+1)
    for i in range(2, N+1):
        lg[i] = lg[i >> 1] + 1
    Q = next(it)
    out = []
    for _ in range(Q):
        L = next(it) - 1
        R = next(it) - 1
        M = R - L + 1
        low = (M + 1) // 2
        high = M - 1
        # binary search
        lo = low
        hi = high
        ans = high + 1
        # local variables for speed
        # Need RMQ inline maybe define nested function? Let's inline in loop? We can define local rmq using st, lg.
        while lo <= hi:
            mid = (lo + hi) // 2
            # compute max g[L .. R-mid]
            left = L
            right = R - mid
            length = right - left + 1
            k = lg[length]
            row = st[k]
            span = 1 << k
            mx1 = row[left]
            mx2 = row[right - span + 1]
            mx = mx1 if mx1 >= mx2 else mx2
            if mx <= mid:
                ans = mid
                hi = mid - 1
            else:
                lo = mid + 1
        if ans <= high:
            out.append(str(M - ans))
        else:
            out.append("0")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

Now, let's think about if we can further optimize RMQ by precomputing `pow2` list to avoid `1 << k` each time. We can have `pow2 = [1<<i for i in range(LOG)]`. Then in query, `span = pow2[k]`. This avoids shift. Also in build. Not necessary but can.

Let's write final code with local variable binding for speed:
def main():
    import sys
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N = int(input())
    A = list(map(int, input().split()))
    g = [0]*N
    p = 0
    for i, a in enumerate(A):
        if p < i + 1:
            p = i + 1
        lim = a * 2
        while p < N and A[p] < lim:
            p += 1
        g[i] = p - i
    del A
    LOG = N.bit_length()
    st = [g]
    pow2 = [1]
    for k in range(1, LOG):
        prev = st[-1]
        half = 1 << (k - 1)
        span = N - (1 << k) + 1
        if span <= 0:
            break
        curr = [a if a >= b else b for a, b in zip(prev, prev[half:])]
        st.append(curr)
        pow2.append(1 << k)
    # But pow2 length should match st. If break early? For N, LOG enough no break except maybe N=1 not. For N=2, LOG=2, k=1 span=1 append pow2[1]=2. Good. If span<=0 for some k? For N=2, k=1 span=1; no break. For N=1 not. So pow2 length LOG. But if N=0 no. Good.
    lg = [0]*(N+1)
    for i in range(2, N+1):
        lg[i] = lg[i >> 1] + 1
    Q = int(input())
    out = []
    st_local = st
    lg_local = lg
    pow2_local = pow2
    for _ in range(Q):
        line = input()
        while line.strip()==b'':? Not needed.
        L, R = map(int, line.split())
        L -= 1; R -= 1
        M = R - L + 1
        lo = (M + 1) // 2
        hi = M - 1
        ans = hi + 1
        while lo <= hi:
            mid = (lo + hi) >> 1
            right = R - mid
            length = right - L + 1
            k = lg_local[length]
            row = st_local[k]
            span = pow2_local[k]
            a = row[L]
            b = row[right - span + 1]
            mx = a if a >= b else b
            if mx <= mid:
                ans = mid
                hi = mid - 1
            else:
                lo = mid + 1
        if ans <= M - 1: # high
            out.append(str(M - ans))
        else:
            out.append("0")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))
Need ensure pow2 list has length LOG. If we build st with break, pow2 append only for built levels. But LOG=N.bit_length() ensures all needed levels built. For N=2, LOG=2, st levels k=0,1; pow2 [1,2]. For N=3, LOG=2, st levels 0,1; pow2 [1,2]. For N=4, LOG=3, levels 0,1,2; pow2 [1,2,4]. Good. If N=2, loop k=1, span=1, append 2. Good. No break. For N=1 (not allowed), LOG=1, loop none, pow2 [1], st [g], lg length2? Actually N+1=2. But N>=2.

But if we use `pow2 = [1]` and append `1 << k` inside loop, for k=1 append2, k=2 append4, etc. Good. However, if `span <=0` break, pow2 would miss levels; not happen for N>=2 with LOG=N.bit_length()? Let's check N=2, LOG=2, k=1 span=1. N=3, LOG=2, k=1 span=2. N=4, LOG=3, k=1 span=3, k=2 span=1. N=5, LOG=3, k=1 span=4, k=2 span=2. So no break. For N=2^m, LOG=m+1, last k=m span=1. Good. So fine.

But wait `LOG = N.bit_length()` for N=2 returns 2, levels k=0,1. For N=3 returns 2, levels k=0,1. For length=3, floor log=1, okay. For N=4 returns 3, levels k=0,1,2. Good. So st length LOG. pow2 length LOG.

Before final, let's further analyze the problem to ensure no alternative interpretation: "There are N mochi, arranged in ascending order of size. The size of the i-th mochi is A_i. Given two mochi A and B, with sizes a and b respectively, you can make one kagamimochi by placing mochi A on top of mochi B if and only if a is at most half of b." So pair condition top size <= bottom size / 2. We used 2*top <= bottom. Good.
"Using only the R_i - L_i + 1 mochi from the L_i-th to the R_i-th, how many kagamimochi can you make simultaneously? choose 2K mochi and form K pairs. For each pair, place one mochi on top of the other." This is maximum matching. Good.

Now, let's think about if the mochi are "arranged in ascending order" but when choosing from L to R, the sizes are sorted. Yes.

Let's maybe derive an alternative direct formula: answer = M - min d. We binary search. Good.

Let's maybe derive an alternative proof of K feasibility via g and d:
For a fixed K, d=M-K. The K smallest are positions L..L+K-1. The K largest are positions R-K+1..R. Pair them in order. The j-th pair is (L+j-1, R-K+j). The gap is d. LOG = N.bit_length()

st = [g]
for k in range(1, LOG):
    prev = st[-1]
    half = pow2[k-1]
    span = N - pow2[k] + 1
    if span <= 0:
        break
    curr = [a if a >= b else b for a, b in zip(prev, prev[half:])]
    st.append(curr)

Two-pointer code:
p = 0
g = [0]*N
for i, a in enumerate(A):
    if p < i + 1:
        p = i + 1
    lim = a * 2
    while p < N and A[p] < lim:
        p += 1
    g[i] = p - i

import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N = int(input())
    A = []
    while len(A) < N:
        A.extend(map(int, input().split()))
    g = [0] * N
    p = 0
    for i, a in enumerate(A):
        if p < i + 1:
            p = i + 1
        lim = a * 2
        while p < N and A[p] < lim:
            p += 1
        g[i] = p - i
    del A

    LOG = N.bit_length()
    pow2 = [1 << i for i in range(LOG)]
    st = [g]
    for k in range(1, LOG):
        prev = st[-1]
        half = pow2[k - 1]
        span = N - pow2[k] + 1
        if span <= 0:
            break
        curr = [a if a >= b else b for a, b in zip(prev, prev[half:])]
        st.append(curr)

    lg = [0] * (N + 1)
    for i in range(2, N + 1):
        lg[i] = lg[i >> 1] + 1

    Q = int(input())
    out = []
    for _ in range(Q):
        L, R = map(int, input().split())
        L -= 1
        R -= 1
        M = R - L + 1
        lo = (M + 1) // 2
        hi = M - 1
        ans = hi + 1
        while lo <= hi:
            mid = (lo + hi) >> 1
            right = R - mid
            length = right - L + 1
            k = lg[length]
            row = st[k]
            span = pow2[k]
            v1 = row[L]
            v2 = row[right - span + 1]
            mx = v1 if v1 >= v2 else v2
            if mx <= mid:
                ans = mid
                hi = mid - 1
            else:
                lo = mid + 1
        if ans <= M - 1:
            out.append(str(M - ans))
        else:
            out.append("0")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Let's maybe write final code cleanly:

```python
import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline

    N = int(input())
    A = []
    while len(A) < N:
        A.extend(map(int, input().split()))

    g = [0] * N
    p = 0
    for i, a in enumerate(A):
        if p < i + 1:
            p = i + 1
        limit = a * 2
        while p < N and A[p] < limit:
            p += 1
        g[i] = p - i
    del A

    LOG = N.bit_length()
    pow2 = [1 << i for i in range(LOG)]

    st = [g]
    for k in range(1, LOG):
        prev = st[-1]
        half = pow2[k - 1]
        span = N - pow2[k] + 1
        if span <= 0:
            break
        curr = [x if x >= y else y for x, y in zip(prev, prev[half:])]
        st.append(curr)

    lg = [0] * (N + 1)
    for i in range(2, N + 1):
        lg[i] = lg[i >> 1] + 1

    Q = int(input())
    out = []
    for _ in range(Q):
        L, R = map(int, input().split())
        L -= 1
        R -= 1
        M = R - L + 1

        lo = (M + 1) // 2
        hi = M - 1
        ans = hi + 1

        while lo <= hi:
            mid = (lo + hi) >> 1
            right = R - mid
            length = right - L + 1
            k = lg[length]
            row = st[k]
            span = pow2[k]
            v1 = row[L]
            v2 = row[right - span + 1]
            mx = v1 if v1 >= v2 else v2

            if mx <= mid:
                ans = mid
                hi = mid - 1
            else:
                lo = mid + 1

        out.append(str(M - ans))

    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This is final.

But g list itself stores references to int objects. The int objects are created by `p - i`; yes 5.6 MB.
- g list refs: 1.6 MB (included in st rows? st[0] is g, so yes included in 26.7? st rows total includes st[0] refs 1.6. Good).
- lg list refs: 1.6 MB, ints small interned no new? Actually `lg[i] = lg[i >> 1] + 1` for values up to17 uses cached small ints, so no per-element int objects. Good.
- pow2 list negligible.
- out list: 2e5 strings. Each string object overhead ~49 bytes + digits ~1, total ~50*2e5=10 MB, list refs 1.6 MB. Total ~11.6 MB.
- A list before del: 1.6 refs + int objects 5.6, but deleted before queries. During st build, A still exists? We del A before st build? In code, `del A` before st build. Good. So during st build, A freed. During queries, no A.
- st list overhead and row lists overhead: 18 lists * (56 + 8*len) included in refs? The 26.7 includes list slots; list object overhead ~18*56=1KB. Good.

- lg list refs: 1.6 MB, ints small interned no new? Actually `lg[i] = lg[i >> 1] + 1` for values up to17 uses cached small ints, so no per-element int objects. Good.
- pow2 list negligible.
- out list: 2e5 strings. Each string object overhead ~49 bytes + digits ~1, total ~50*2e5=10 MB, list refs 1.6 MB. Total ~11.6 MB.
- A list before del: 1.6 refs + int objects 5.6, but deleted before queries. During st build, A still exists? We del A before st build? In code, `del A` before st build. Good. So during st build, A freed. During queries, no A.

=== ANSWER ===
.</think>```python
import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline

    N = int(input())
    A = []
    while len(A) < N:
        A.extend(map(int, input().split()))

    # g[i] = minimum gap d such that A[i+d] >= 2*A[i]
    g = [0] * N
    p = 0
    for i, a in enumerate(A):
        if p < i + 1:
            p = i + 1
        limit = a * 2
        while p < N and A[p] < limit:
            p += 1
        g[i] = p - i
    del A

    # Sparse table for range maximum of g
    LOG = N.bit_length()
    pow2 = [1 << i for i in range(LOG)]

    st = [g]
    for k in range(1, LOG):
        prev = st[-1]
        half = pow2[k - 1]
        span = N - pow2[k] + 1
        if span <= 0:
            break
        curr = [x if x >= y else y for x, y in zip(prev, prev[half:])]
        st.append(curr)

    lg = [0] * (N + 1)
    for i in range(2, N + 1):
        lg[i] = lg[i >> 1] + 1

    Q = int(input())
    out = []

    for _ in range(Q):
        L, R = map(int, input().split())
        L -= 1
        R -= 1

        M = R - L + 1
        lo = (M + 1) // 2
        hi = M - 1
        ans = hi + 1

        while lo <= hi:
            mid = (lo + hi) >> 1
            right = R - mid
            length = right - L + 1

            k = lg[length]
            row = st[k]
            span = pow2[k]

            v1 = row[L]
            v2 = row[right - span + 1]
            mx = v1 if v1 >= v2 else v2

            if mx <= mid:
                ans = mid
                hi = mid - 1
            else:
                lo = mid + 1

        out.append(str(M - ans))

    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
